§ 125-32. Standards.
When locating new Intensely Developed or Limited Development Areas, the following
standards shall apply.
A.
Except as may be provided in subsection (d) below, locate a new Intensely
Developed Area (IDA) in an existing Limited Development Area (LDA) or
adjacent to an existing IDA;
Except as may be provided in subsection (d) below, locate a new LDA adjacent to
an existing LDA or IDA;
Except as may be provided in D below, a new IDA shall be at least 20 acres.
The County Council recognizes that the County may not be able to utilize growth
allocation acreage in the locations set forth in subsections A and B above and/or
may not satisfy the 20-acre size threshold set forth in subsection C above, and
further recognizes that the majority of its critical area lands for which growth
allocation is planned are currently classified as Resource Conservation Areas.
Accordingly, in the event that the County is unable to satisfy any or all of the
criteria set forth in subsections A-C, the County may utilize a portion of its growth
allocation in a manner that varies from subsections A-C above, provided that the
area to receive growth allocation meets the following standards:
(1)
Any development will be serviced by public water and sewer or septic
system that uses the best available nitrogen removal technology;
The area is located in a Priority
Funding Area;
The development will have an overall economic benefit to the area, or
Implements a specific goal, objective, or policy of the Wicomico County
43

<!-- page 168 -->
Comprehensive Plan; or
(5)
Is a commercial marina or restaurant in existence since October 13, 1989.
No more than 1/2 of the growth allocated may be located in the Resource
Conservation Area (RCA), except as provided in Subsection F below.
If Wicomico County is unable to utilize any portion of the Growth Allocation
within or adjacent to existing IDAs or LDAs, that portion of the Growth Allocation
that cannot be used may be located in the RCA. The developer will be required to
cluster. The developer shall cluster the development 300 feet from tidal waters or
tidal wetlands.
The location of development projects that use Growth Allocation shall minimize
impacts to Habitat Protection Areas (HPAs) as defined in
COMAR 27.01.09 and as
provided in Articles IV and V of this Chapter and optimize benefits to water
quality. The design of development projects that utilize the Growth Allocation shall
minimize impacts to Habitat Protection Areas (HPAs), as defined in COMAR
27.01.09 and as provided in Article II of this Chapter, and optimize benefits to
water quality.
Projects using Growth Allocation in the RCA shall conform to all criteria of this
Chapter for such areas. The Growth Allocation used shall be designated on
Wicomico County Critical Area District Maps and shall constitute an amendment
to this Chapter, subject to review and approval by the Wicomico County Planning
Commission, Wicomico County governing body, and the Critical Area
Commission.
Locate a new IDA or an LDA in an RCA at least 300 feet beyond the landward
edge of tidal wetlands or tidal waters.
Locate a new IDA where it will minimize impacts on the defined land uses of the
RCA.
Locate new Intensely Developed Areas and Limited Development Areas outside of
areas vulnerable to climate change as identified by the County, unless the County
proposes and the Commission approves measures that assess climate resiliency and
vulnerability and incorporate siting, design, construction, and other natural features
to enhance climate resiliency and reduce vulnerability significantly.
§ 125-33. Factors.
In reviewing map amendments or refinements involving the use of growth allocation, the County
shall provide the Commission with all information and documentation that addresses the
following factors:

Consistency with the County’s adopted comprehensive plan and whether the growth
allocation would implement the goals and objectives of the adopted plan.
For amap amendment or refinement involving a new Intensely Developed Area, whether

(1)
To be served by a public wastewater system;
(2)
Have an allowed average density of at least 3.5 units per acre as calculated under
State Finance and Procurement Article, §5-7B-03(h), Annotated Code of
Maryland;
(3)
If greater than 20 acres, be located in a priority funding area; and
(4)
Have a demonstrable economic benefit to the area.
For a map amendment or refinement involving a new Limited Development Area,
whether the development is:
(1)
To be served by a public wastewater system.or septic system that
uuses the best
available nitrogen removal technology;
(2)
Completion of an existing subdivision;
(3)
An expansion of an existing business; or
(4)
To be clustered.
The use of existing public infrastructure, where practical;
Consistency with State and regional environmental protection policies concerning the
protection of threatened and endangered species and species in need of conservation that
may be located on- or off-site;
Impacts on a priority preservation area, as defined under § 2-518 of the Agriculture
Article;
Environmental impacts associated with wastewater and stormwater management practices
and wastewater and stormwater discharges to tidal waters, tidal wetlands, and tributary
streams; and
Environmental impacts associated with a location in a coastal hazard area or an increased
risk of severe flooding attributable to the proposed development.
Environmental impacts on underserved or overburdened communities.

For the number of acres approved within a growth allocation envelope, if:

Areas that support the proposed development are included within the

When converting:

A resource conservation area, the remaining resource conservation

20 acres, or
From a limited development area to a new intensely developed

The County may not approve a growth allocation development envelope in the RCA
unless the County determines, based on subdivision and development history, that given

(4)

(5)
The acreage of tidal wetlands on the parcel.
The County shall authorize a maximum of one growth allocation envelope per parcel
unless;

Deduction of more than one growth allocation envelope will provide a water
quality or habitat benefit and
A)
The Commission approves more than one growth allocation envelope.

Article VII. Vesting.
§ 125-35. Continuation of existing uses.
A.
The continuation, but not necessarily the intensification or expansion, of any use in
existence on October 13, 1989, may be allowed unless the use has been abandoned for
more than one year or is otherwise restricted by existing ordinances.
If any existing use does not conform with the provisions of this Ordinance, its
intensification or expansion may be authorized only in accordance with the variance
procedures in Article XI (Variance).
§ 125-36. Residential density.
.”
Except as otherwise provided, the following types of land may be developed with a single-family

A.
Any land on which development activity has progressed to the point of pouring of

A legal parcel of land, not being part of a recorded or approved subdivision that was

Land that received a building permit subsequent to December 1, 1985, but prior to

Land that was subdivided into recorded, legally buildable lots, where the subdivision

A.
For purposes of implementing this regulation, a local jurisdiction shall have determined,
based on land uses and development in existence on December 1, 1985, which land areas
fall within the three types of development areas described in this Chapter.

Nothing in this Section may be interpreted as altering any requirements of this Ordinance

Article VIII. Critical Area Amendments.
§ 125-38. Amendment regulations generally.
A.
The County Council may, from time to time, amend the provisions of this Chapter, but
not more than four (4) times per calendar year, and the land use management
classification of properties in the Critical Area District. In addition, the County Council
shall review and propose any necessary amendments, as required, to the land use
management classifications in the Critical Area District at least every ten (10) years.
All such amendments shall be approved by the Chesapeake and Coastal Bays Critical
Area Commission pursuant to § 8-1809(g) of the Critical Area Law, Subtitle 18.
Standards for Critical Area Commission approval ofproposed amendments are as set
forth in the Critical Area Law, Subtitle 18, § 8-1809(h) and (1). The Critical Area
Commission process for approval of proposed amendments is as set forth in the Critical
Area Law, Subtitle 18, § 8-1809(d).
§ 125-39. Comprehensive reviews.
The County shall review Chapter 125 and propose any necessary amendments to this Chapter at
least every ten (10) years in accordance with Natural Resources Article, §8-1809 (g). The
anniversary of the date that Chapter 125 became effective shall be used to determine when the
review shall be completed. Within sixty (60) days after the completion of the review, the County
shall send the following information in writing to the Commission:
A.
A statement certifying that the required review has been accomplished.
B.
All necessary requests for Chapter 125 amendments, refinements, or other matters that
the County wishes the Commission to consider.
An updated resource inventory.
A statement quantifying acreages within each land classification, the growth allocation
used, the growth allocation remaining, anda list of changes to be made.
§ 125-40. Modified Buffer Areas amendments.
The Wicomico County Council may designate a Buffer Management Area (MBA) as per § 125-
24 only by Chapter amendment, where it can be sufficiently demonstrated that the existing
pattern of residential, industrial, commercial, or recreational development in the Critical Area
District portion of the site prevents the Buffer from performing its function. All such
amendments shall require a public hearing by the Planning Commission and the County Council,
as well as approval by the Critical Area Commission. In order to approve a Buffer exemption
48

<!-- page 173 -->
amendment, the County Council shall find and require, at
a minimum, that other measures for
achieving the habitat and water quality protection objectives of the adopted Critical Area District
criteria have been met.
§ 125-41. Amendment procedures.
A.
Proposed amendments to the text provisions of this Chapter may only be initiated by the
Wicomico County Planning
Commission or the Wicomico County Council.
All Chapter amendments and any map amendments involving specific properties
requested by the property owner shall be submitted to the Wicomico County Department
of Planning, Zoning, and Community Development.
For all Chapter amendments, the Wicomico County Planning and Zoning Commission
shall first hold a public hearing related thereto, at which parties of interest and citizens
shall have an opportunity to be heard. At least 15 days' notice of the time and place of
such hearing shall be published in a newspaper of general circulation in Wicomico
County. For amendments involving only one specific property, the Wicomico County
Planning and Zoning Commission shall post notice of its public hearing on the
property(ies) for which amendments are requested and, to the extent possible based on
the best available information, notify all property owners immediately contiguous to the
applicant of the hearing date, time, and place. The Wicomico County Planning and
Zoning Commission shall also furnish these property owners with a copy of said
application.
The Wicomico County Planning and Zoning Commission shall then forward proposed
amendments to the Wicomico County Council. The Council shall hold a public hearing
on the amendments and, if approved, shall submit said amendment(s) to the Critical Area
Commission.
After receiving the approval of the Critical Area Commission, the County Council may,
at its discretion, hold a second public hearing on the ordinance enacting the proposed
amendment. At this time, parties in interest and citizens shall have an opportunity to be
heard. Notice of the time and place of such public hearing shall be published in a
newspaper of general circulation in Wicomico County once each week for two
consecutive weeks.
Requirements for amendments.
(i)
Critical Area District Map amendments. Except for Chapter amendments or

mistake if the proposed Critical Area classification:

Conforms to the State Critical Area mapping criteria.

on land uses or natural features in existence at the time of the remapping.
Follows the County’s documented mapping methodology for Critical Area
classifications at the time of the original program adoption.
Is consistent with the purposes, policies, and goals of this subtitle and all
criteria of the Critical Area Commission.
(e)
Is approved by the Critical Area Commission.
A Critical Area District Map Amendment may be granted if the zoning map

Proposes the use of a part of the remaining growth allocation in

Article [X. Lot consolidation and reconfiguration.
§ 125-42. Applicability.
The provisions of this Section apply to a consolidation or a reconfiguration of any
nonconforming legally grandfathered parcel or lot. These provisions do not apply to the
reconfiguration or consolidation of parcels or lots that are conforming and meet all the
requirements of the Critical Area District. Nonconforming parcels or lots include:
A.
Those for which a Critical Area variance is sought or has been issued; and
B.
Those located in the Resource Conservation Area District (RCA) and are less than 20
acres in Size.

<!-- page 175 -->
§ 125-43. Procedure.
A.
E.
An applicant seeking a parcel or lot consolidation or reconfiguration shall provide the
required information in § 200-9 of the Wicomico County Subdivision Chapter and
subsection D below to Wicomico County.
Wicomico County may not approve a proposed parcel or lot consolidation or
reconfiguration without making written findings in accordance with § 125-45 below and
COMAR 27.01.02.08.F.
Wicomico County shall issue a final written decision or order granting or denying an
application for consolidation or reconfiguration.
After a final written decision or order is issued, Wicomico County shall send a copy of
the decision or order and a copy of any approved development plan within ten (10)
business days by U.S. mail to the Critical Area Commission’s business address.
Wicomico County may not issue a building permit until the appeal time has expired.
"
§ 125-44. Application.
An application for the consolidation or reconfiguration of any nonconforming legal parcel of
land or recorded legally buildable lot shall contain at least the following information:
A.
The date of recordation of each legal parcel of land or legally buildable lot to be
consolidated or reconfigured;
A plan drawn to scale that shows all existing and proposed lot or parcel boundaries as per
§ 200 of the Wicomico County Subdivision Chapter;
Information sufficient for Wicomico County to make the findings set forth in Chapter 200
of the Wicomico County Subdivision Chapter and subsection D below; and
A table that lists the number of all legal parcels of land or recorded legally buildable lots
and the number ofproposed lots or parcels, or dwelling units to be derived.
§ 125-45. Standards.
The Planning
Commission shall review a proposed lot consolidation or reconfiguration and will
make written findings that each of the following standards has been met:
A.
The proposed consolidation or reconfiguration will result in no greater number of lots,
parcels, or dwelling units in the Critical Area District than the existing configuration
would allow;
The proposed lot consolidation or reconfiguration will result in no greater lot coverage
than the existing configuration would allow;

<!-- page 176 -->
The proposed consolidation or reconfiguration does not:
(1)
Create an additional riparian lot or parcel, waterfront lot, or any other lot or parcel
deeded with water access; or
(2)
Intensify or increase impacts associated with riparian access;
The proposed consolidation or reconfiguration does not create:
(1)
A lot or parcel, or portion of a lot or parcel that will serve development activities
outside the Critical Area District or
(2)
An RCA lot or parcel that serves development activities in the Intensely
Developed Area District (IDA) or Limited Development Area District (LDA);
The proposed consolidation or reconfiguration identifies each Habitat Protection Area
(HPA), and if impacts to an HPA are proposed, the proposal demonstrates that:
(i)
No greater impact on an HPA would result than the impact that would have
resulted from the existing lot configuration;
Adverse impacts on an HPA are minimized; and
(3)
Protective measures and restoration measures are included that provide for the
least possible impact.
The proposed consolidation or reconfiguration provides:
(1)
Stormwater management for all proposed development activities; and
(2)
Benefits to fish, wildlife, and plant habitats that are clearly identified.
The proposed consolidation or reconfiguration fully complies with the afforestation and
reforestation requirements in COMAR 27.01.05 and 27.01.09, unless clearing is
necessary to avoid a habitat protection area.
Article X. Intrafamily transfers.
§ 125-46. Intrafamily transfers.
The one (1) unit per 20-acre density limitation shall not prevent a bona fide intrafamily transfer
to members of the owner's immediate family, subject to the following limitations:
A.
Intrafamily transfers will be permitted on parcels of land in the Critical Area District
where it is shown that the parcel was recorded on or before March 1, 1986, and such
parcel is at least seven (7) acres and not more than 60 acres in size.
A bona fide intrafamily transfer shall be subject to all requirements of the Wicomico
County Subdivision Regulations,[1] and a notation shall be placed on the final
subdivision plat denoting the lot(s) that are created under these provisions.
52

<!-- page 177 -->
Subdivision of land under the bona fide intrafamily transfer provisions contained herein
shall be subject to the following limitations:
(1)
Parcels of seven (7) acres to less than 12 acres cannot be subdivided into more
than a total of two (2) lots.
Parcels of 12 acres to less than 60 acres cannot be subdivided into more than three
(3) lots.
(3)
The lots may be created at different times.
Lots created pursuant to these provisions shall not be created for purposes of ultimate
commercial sale. A lot created pursuant to these provisions may not be subsequently
conveyed to any person except as provided herein:
(1)
Where the conveyance is to
a member of the owner's immediate family; or
(2) | Where the conveyance of the lot is as part of a default on a mortgage or deed of
trust.
Conditions of approval.
As a condition of approval, the County shall require that:
(1)
Any deed for a lot that is created by a bona fide intrafamily transfer shall contain
a covenant approved by the County Attorney stating that the lot is created subject
to the provisions of Natural Resources Article Chapter 8-1808.2, Annotated Code
of Maryland, and
(2)
A lot created by a bona fide intrafamily transfer may not be conveyed
subsequently to any person other than
a member of the owner's immediate family
except as provided in F below.
(3)
This Chapter does not prevent the conveyance of the lot to a third party as
security for a mortgage or deed of trust.
Any lot created under this section may not be transferred or sold to a third party who is
not a member of the owner's immediate family or holder of a mortgage or deed of trust on
the property unless and until the Wicomico County Planning
and Zoning Commission
has determined that the following conditions apply:
(1)
A change in circumstances has occurred since the original transfer, not of the
owner's own doing, which would warrant permitting a subsequent transfer when
such circumstances are consistent with the warrants and exceptions contained
herein; or
Other circumstances necessary to maintain land areas to support protective uses of
agriculture, forestry, open space, and natural habitats in RCA warrant an
exception.

<!-- page 178 -->
Article XI. Variances.
§ 125-47. Authorization.
The Wicomico County Board ofAppeals is hereby empowered to grant variances to the
provisions of this Chapter where, owing to special features of a site or other circumstances, a
literal enforcement of provisions would result in unwarranted hardship.
A.
In considering an application for a variance, Wicomico County shall presume that the
specific development activity in the Critical Area that is subject to the application and for
which a variance is required does not conform with the general purpose and intent of the
Natural Resources Article, Title 8, Subtitle 18, COMAR Title 27, and the requirements of
this Chapter.
Unwarranted hardship means that, without a variance, an applicant would be denied
reasonable and significant use of the entire parcel or lot for which the variance is
requested.
§ 125-48. Standing.
In accordance with Natural Resources Article, §8-1808(d)(2), Annotated Code ofMaryland, if a
person meets the threshold standing requirements under federal law, the person shall have
standing to participate as a party in a local administrative proceeding.
§ 125-49. Standards.
The Board ofAppeals shall examine all facts of the case and render a decision. Variance requests
in the Critical Area District shall not be granted unless the applicant has satisfied each of the
following standards:
A.
Due to special features of the site or special conditions or circumstances peculiar to the
land or structure involved, a literal enforcement of provisions and requirements of this
Critical Area Ordinance would result in an unwarranted hardship.
A literal interpretation of the provisions of this Chapter will deprive the applicant of the
use of land or a structure permitted to others in accordance with the provisions of this
Chapter;
That the granting of a variance will not confer upon an applicant any special privilege
that would be denied to other owners of like property and/or structures within the Critical
Area District.
That the variance request is not based upon conditions or circumstances that are self-
created or self-imposed, including the commencement of development activity before an
application for a variance has been filed;

<!-- page 179 -->
The variance request does not arise from conditions or circumstances, either permitted or
nonconforming, which are related to adjacent parcels.
That the granting of a variance will not adversely affect water quality or adversely impact
fish, wildlife, or plant habitat within the Critical Area District and that the granting of the
variance will be consistent with the spirit and intent of this Chapter and associated
Chapters.
The granting of the variance will be in harmony with the general spirit and intent of the
State Critical Area Law and this Chapter.
That greater profitability or lack ofknowledge of the restrictions shall not be considered
as sufficient cause for a variance.
Other considerations.
(1)
The applicant has the burden ofproof and the burden of persuasion to prove they
meet all the required standards.
Wicomico County will carefully review all the evidence presented and create a
written document detailing its findings as to whether the applicant successfully
met the required standards, using competent and substantial evidence.
The evidence used to make the decision can come from:
(a)
The applicant.
(b)
Wicomico County or other government agencies.
(c)
Any other individuals that Wicomico County considers relevant.
§ 125-50. Process.
A.
Applications for a variance will be made in writing to the Wicomico County Board of
Appeals, with a copy provided to the Critical Area Commission. The County shall follow
its established procedures for advertising and notification of affected landowners.
After hearing an application for a Critical Area Program variance, the Board ofAppeals
shall make written findings reflecting an analysis of each standard. With due regard for
the person’s experience, technical competence, and specialized knowledge, the written
findings may be based on evidence introduced and testimony presented by:
(1)
The applicant;
(2)
The County or any other government agency; or
(3)
Any other person deemed appropriate by the County.

<!-- page 180 -->
If the variance request is based on conditions or circumstances that are the result of
actions by the applicant, the County shall consider that fact and whether the application
has met the requirements of § 125-52.
The applicant has the burden ofproof and the burden ofpersuasion to overcome the
presumption of nonconformance established in § 125-47.
The County shall notify the Critical Area Commission of its findings and decision to grant
or deny the variance request.
§ 125-51. After-the-fact requests.
A.
Wicomico County will not accept an application for a variance to legalize a violation,
including an unpermitted or otherwise authorized structure or other development activity,
until the County:
(1)
Issues a notice of violation; and
(s)
_, Assesses an administrative or civil penalty for the violation.
The County will not issue a permit, approval, variance, or special exception to legalize a
violation of this subtitle unless an applicant has:
Fully paid all penalties imposed under Natural Resources Article, §8-1808(c)(1),
Annotated Code of Maryland;
Prepared a restoration or mitigation plan, approved by the local jurisdiction, to
abate impacts to water quality or natural resources as a result of the violation; and
Performed the abatement measures in the approved plan in accordance with this
Chapter.
If the Board denies the requested after-the-fact variance, then the County shall:
(1)
Order removal or relocation of any structure; and
(2)
Order restoration of the affected resources.
§ 125-52. Appeals.
A.
Appeals from a decision concerning the granting or denial of a variance under these
regulations shall be taken in accordance with all applicable laws and procedures of the
County for variances.
Variance decisions by the Board ofAppeals may be appealed to the Circuit Court in
accordance with the Maryland Rules of Procedure.

<!-- page 181 -->
Appeals may be taken by any person, firm, corporation, or governmental agency
aggrieved or adversely affected by any decision made under this Chapter or any person
with standing as described in § 125-48 above.
The County may not issue a permit or any other type of authorization until the applicable
30-day appeal period has expired.
§ 125-53. Conditions and mitigation.
The Board ofAppeals shall impose conditions on the use or development of a property that is
granted a variance as it may find reasonable to ensure that the spirit and intent of this Chapter are
maintained, including, but not limited to, the following:
A.
Adverse impacts resulting from the granting of the variance shall be mitigated as
recommended by the Planning and Zoning Commission, but not less than by planting on
the site per square foot of the variance granted at no less than a three-to-one basis.
New or expanded structures or lot coverage shall be located at the greatest possible
distance from mean high water, the landward edge of tidal wetlands, tributary streams,
nontidal wetlands, or steep slopes.
§ 125-54. Commission notification.
Within ten (10) working days after a written decision regarding a variance application is issued, a
copy of the decision will be sent to the Critical Area Commission.

Article XII. Transfer of development rights.
§ 125-56. Definition.
A.
Transfer of development rights is the conveyance of development rights by deed,
easement, or other legal instrument authorized by local law to another parcel of land and
the recordation of that conveyance among the land records of Wicomico County,
Maryland.
§ 125-57. Right of transfer.
A.
In general. Transfer of Development Rights (TDR) allows a property owner to sell their
development rights, thereby separating these rights from the bundle of rights associated
with land ownership. A development right of a transferor parcel may be transferred and
used to increase residential or nonresidential development on a receiving parcel in
accordance with the provisions of this section.
/’ The TDR option allows the landowner to potentially double the density of a cluster
subdivision by transferring additional development rights to the subdivision from another
Resource Conservation Area District (RCA) property.
Limitations.
(1)
A development right may not be used in any manner inconsistent with the
provisions set forth in this section.
A development right may not be used to increase density for receiving parcels
located within the Critical Area RCA beyond the density allowed within the
parcel's zoning district.
The property from which the additional development rights are transferred must
place a reservation of development rights on 20 acres for every development right
that is transferred from the RCA in accordance with the RCA density limitations.
Intermediate transfer. Subject to the provisions of § 125-59 below, a development
right may be transferred to a transferee prior to the time when its use for a specific
receiving parcel has been finally approved in accordance with this Chapter.
§ 125-58. Eligibility Criteria.
A.
Eligible sending areas are properties vulnerable to climate-related hazards, including sea-
level rise, flooding, storm surges, or erosion, where natural features (e.g., wetlands,
forests, coastal ecosystems) or human systems (e.g., infrastructure, communities) are at
risk of damage. These areas have limited capacity to adapt, recover, or mitigate the
impacts of climate change due to constraints such as geographic location, economic
resources, or ecological limitations. The climate resilience analysis (as outlined in
62

<!-- page 187 -->
subsections C and D) must demonstrate these vulnerabilities quantitatively, indicating
that approved transfers will reduce long-term exposure to hazards, protect sensitive
ecosystems, and equitably distribute adaptation benefits by preserving sending areas for
conservation or low-impact uses.
Eligible receiving areas are properties with low or no vulnerability to climate-related
hazards, such as sea-level rise, flooding, storm surges, or erosion. These areas feature
resilient natural features (e.g., uplands, stable soils, non-sensitive ecosystems) and human
systems (e.g., infrastructure, communities) capable of withstanding climate change
impacts. Eligible receiving areas have sufficient adaptive capacity to support
development without significant ecological or societal risk, bolstered by factors such as a
favorable geographic location (e.g., higher elevation, inland areas), robust economic
resources, or ecological stability. Designating receiving areas aims to foster sustainable
development in locations that can safely accommodate increased development while
minimizing environmental harm and climate-related risks. The climate resilience analysis
(as outlined in subsections C and D) must verify this resilience through quantifiable
metrics, implying that approved transfers will enhance overall Critical Area adaptation by
directing development to low-risk zones, reducing potential economic losses from future
hazards, and supporting equitable community resilience.
In addition to the requirements for TDRs in Chapter 225, Section 225-58, applications for
TDRs under this section must include a climate resiliency analysis based on the climate
resilience criteria outlined in subsection D, demonstrating that sending and receiving
properties meet the eligibility criteria outlined in subsections A and B. The analysis's
implications include guiding the Planning Director's certification and Planning
Commission's approval decisions (§ 125-60 and § 125-64), where failure to meet
quantifiable thresholds may result in application denial or required modifications.
Positive findings imply strengthened climate adaptation in the RCA, reduced
vulnerability for human systems (e.g., underserved communities), and alignment with
state mandates on environmental justice and resilience (e.g., SB0306, 2024 Maryland
General Assembly).
Climate resilience criteria (quantifiable where possible).
(1)
Project outside 0-2 foot sea level rise inundation zones (per MD Commission on
Climate Change projections) unless elevated >2 feet above base flood elevation
(Base Flood Elevation). Implication: Ensures long-term habitability, implying
reduced relocation costs and preserved property values in sending areas while
enabling safe density increases in receiving areas.
No increase in flood risks (e.g., within 100-year floodplain, must comply with
500-year standards if in a vulnerable area). Implication: Prevents the
amplification of downstream flooding, resulting in lower insurance premiums,
minimized community disruption, and equitable protection for vulnerable
populations.
63

<!-- page 188 -->
Minimizes erosion: In areas with >2 feet/year historical erosion (per DNR maps),
use living shorelines or permeable materials for >50% new coverage. Implication:
Stabilizes shorelines and ecosystems, implying enhanced biodiversity, reduced
sediment pollution, and cost savings on future erosion control measures.
Incorporates resilient design (e.g., native, flood-tolerant vegetation in mitigation;
elevated structures to withstand Category 2 storm surge). Implication: Builds
adaptive capacity into development, implying decreased damage from extreme
events, improved ecological services, and support for sustainable growth without
environmental degradation.
Demonstration via site plan showing consideration of 2-5 foot Sea Level Rise
over project lifespan (50-100 years). Evidence includes applicant submissions,
County/agency input, and expert testimony. Implication: Future-proof
investments, implying alignment with long-term climate goals, avoid economic
losses from maladaptation, and equitable benefits through proactive planning for
all stakeholders.
§ 125-59. Effect of transfer.
After an original instrument of transfer has transferred development rights:
A.
The transferor parcel:
(1)
(2)
(3)
May not be subdivided or reconfigured;
Shall be used only for agricultural uses, other than farm residences; and
May not be used in connection with any determination of site area or site capacity,
except as may be necessary in determining the number of development rights
involved in the transfer; and
All development rights that are the subject of the transfer, and the value of such rights,
shall be deemed for all other purposes, including assessment and taxation, to be
appurtenant to the transferor parcel until such rights have been finally approved for use
on a specific receiving parcel and transferred to the County Council.
§ 125-60. Certificate of Planning Director.
A.
General requirement. A transfer may not be recognized under this Chapter unless the
original instrument of transfer:
(1)
Contains a certificate of the Planning Director that the number of development
rights that are the subject of the transfer represents the number of development
rights applicable to the transferor parcel and
Is recorded by the Planning Director as provided in this Chapter.
64

<!-- page 189 -->
Responsibility. The transferor and the transferee named in an original instrument of
transfer shall have sole responsibility to:
(1)
Supply all information required by this section.
(2)
To provide a proper original instrument of transfer; and
(3)
To pay, in addition to any other fees required by this section, all costs of its
recordation among the land records ofWicomico County.
Application for certificate. An application for a certificate shall:
(1)
Contain information prescribed by the Planning Director, as may be necessary to
determine the number of development rights involved in the proposed transfer;
(2)
Include five copies of a plat of the proposed transferor parcel prepared by a
registered land surveyor on the basis of an actual on-site survey and
(3)
Be accompanied by such fee(s) as may be prescribed by the County Council.
On the basis of the information submitted, the Planning Director shall affix a certificate
of the Planning Director's findings to the original instrument of transfer. The certificate
shall contain a specific statement of the number of development rights derived from the
transferred parcel.
Effect of determination. The determination of the Planning Director may not be construed
to enlarge or otherwise affect in any manner the nature, character, and effect of a transfer,
as set forth in § 125-59 of this subsection.

In general. An instrument of transfer shall conform to the requirements of this section.
An instrument of transfer, other than an original instrument oftransfer, need not contain a
metes and bounds description or plat of the transferor parcel.

County covenants.

Application. The owner of a proposed receiving parcel shall file with the Planning

§ 125-64. Consideration of the application for use.
A.
Review of application. The Planning Director shall review the instruments of transfer or
agreement submitted with the application and determine their sufficiency to fulfill the
requirements of this Chapter.
Determination. The Planning Director shall:
(1)
Determine the number of development rights that are available for use under the
terms of the instruments submitted with the application, incorporating findings
from the climate resiliency analysis on vulnerability and adaptive capacity.
(4)
Determine the number of development rights that this section allows to be used on

(3)
Report the preliminary determination of the Planning Director in writing to the
applicant within thirty (30) days after all information necessary to make the
determination has been received.
c
The Planning Commission must approve a request to utilize development rights if the
request:
(1)
Does not exceed the limitation on the density or number of dwelling units
permitted in the zone and the applicable comprehensive plan;
(2)
Is in accordance with the provisions of this Chapter, including the climate
resiliency analysis findings under § 125-58D.
(3)
Is in accordance with Chapter 200, Titled "Subdivision of Land";
Is consistent with other recommendations of the comprehensive plan; and
Achieves a desirable development compatible with both site conditions and the
surrounding existing and future development, as supported by the analysis's
implications for long-term climate resilience and equity (e.g., protecting
vulnerable communities from displaced risks).
Effect. The Planning Director's determination is not final and may be amended, modified,
or rescinded until the transfer is finalized per § 125-65. If the analysis identifies
unresolved risks, the Commission may condition approval on modifications or deny the
request to ensure alignment with Critical Area adaptation goals.
§ 125-65. Final approval of use.
A.
When final. Transfer from a transferor parcel to a receiving parcel is final at the time
when:

Final subdivision approval or final site plan approval with respect to the receiving
parcel, based upon the use of development rights, has been given; and

All development rights upon which such approval was based have been
transferred to Wicomico County as provided in Subsection B of this section.
Required instruments. Final approval may not be given to any site plan or subdivision
plan that involves the use of development rights transferred under the provisions of this
section until satisfactory evidence is presented that such of the following instruments as
may be required to effect the transfer of those rights to Wicomico County have been

(4)
An instrument of transfer from the owner of the receiving parcel to Wicomico
County.
Incorporation of Climate Resiliency Analysis Findings: Final subdivision or site plans
must incorporate the findings and recommendations from the climate resiliency analysis
(§ 125-58C), such as resilient design features (e.g., elevated structures, permeable
surfaces) and mitigation measures. The Planning Director shall verify compliance prior to
approval, with implications including enforceable covenants in recorded instruments or
post-approval inspections to ensure implementation. Non-incorporation may result in
revocation of approval or denial of building permits.
§ 125-66. Reserved.
Article XIV. Water-dependent facilities.
§ 125-67. Applicability.
A.
The provisions of this Chapter apply to those structures or works associated with
industrial, maritime, recreational, educational, or fisheries activities that require location
at or near the shoreline within the Buffer.
The provisions of this Chapter are not applicable to:
(1)
A private pier that:
(a)
Is installed or maintained by a riparian landowner; and
(b)
Is not part of a residential project that provides
a community pier or other
community boat-docking or storage facility under Regulation .07 of this
Chapter or

(2)
A nonwater-dependent project covered under COMAR 27.01.13.
C.
The requirements of COMAR 27.01.02 apply to this Chapter.
§ 125-68. General Criteria.
The following standards shall! apply to all new or expanded development activities
associated with water-dependent facilities:
A.
In accordance with Natural Resources Article §8-1808.3, Annotated Code of
Maryland, permitted development in the Buffer is limited to the minimum lot
coverage necessary to accommodate each water-dependent facility or activity.
New or expanded development activities may be permitted in the Buffer in the
Intensely Developed Areas and Limited Development Areas, provided that it can be
shown:
(1)
That the facility or activity is water-dependent, .
FA
That the facility or activity meets a recognized private right or public need;
(3)
That adverse effects on water quality, fish, wildlife, and plant habitats are
first avoided, or if unavoidable, minimized;
(4)
That, insofar as possible, a non-water-dependent project associated with the
water-dependent facility or activity is located outside the Buffer;
(5)
Impacts to fish, wildlife, or plant habitat are avoided, or if unavoidable,
minimized; and
()
Mitigation is provided at a minimum ratio of 1:1 based on the square
footage of canopy coverage removed.
Except as otherwise authorized in this section, a water-dependent facility or activity is
prohibited in the Buffer of the Resource Conservation Area.
The placement of dredged material in the Buffer or a portion of the Critical Area that has
been designated as a habitat protection area is prohibited, except as necessary for:
(1)
A beneficial use approved by the Board of Public Works or the Department of the
Environment, such as:
(a)
Backfill for a shoreline stabilization measure;
(b)
Use in a nonstructural shoreline stabilization measure, includinga living
shoreline;
(a)
Beach nourishment;

<!-- page 197 -->
Restoration of an island;
()
The creation, restoration, or enhancement of a wetland ora fish, wildlife,
or plant habitat; or
(f)
Any other approved beneficial use; or
Placement in an area that was approved for the disposal of channel maintenance
dredged material before October 13, 1989.
§ 125-69. General requirements for the location of water-dependent facilities or activities.
A.
The County shall evaluate on a case-by-case basis all proposals for expansion of existing
or new water-dependent facilities. The County shall work with, as applicable, the
Departments ofAgriculture, the Environment, and Natural Resources, and any other
appropriate federal or State agency to develop a plan for the approval of an area suitable
for the location of a new or expanded water-dependent facility or activity.
The County shall document that each of the following siting factors is accorded due
consideration when evaluating proposals for new or expanded water-dependent facilities:
(1)
The impact on the water body upon which the water-dependent facility or activity
is proposed that would likely result from the approval of that location, including:
(a)
Alteration of an existing water circulation pattern or salinity regime;
(())
Adequacy of area flushing characteristics;
()
Necessity of, and proximity to, a dredging operation; and
(d)
Interference with the natural transport of sand;
Disturbance to:
(a)
An oyster harvest area, as defined in COMAR 08.02.04.11;
(b)
An area covered in a current aquaculture lease, as defined in Natural
Resources Article, §4-11A-01, Annotated Code of Maryland;
()
A harvest reserve area, as designated under Natural Resources Article, §4-
1009.1, Annotated Code of Maryland;
An oyster sanctuary, as established in COMAR 08.02.04.15A and
(e)
Any other shellfish located in a shellfish area regulated by the Department
of Natural Resources;
Avoidance of disturbance to water quality and aquatic or terrestrial habitat
resulting from the method or manner of dredging; and
The avoidance or, if avoidance is not possible, the minimization of:
71

<!-- page 198 -->
Disturbance to:
wetland;
Submerged aquatic vegetation;
A habitat of threatened or endangered species or species in need of
conservation;
In accordance with COMAR 26.08.02.04-2, a water body
identified by the Department of the Environment as a Tier I, high-
quality water body and its watershed;
In accordance with COMAR 26.08.02.04-3, a water body
identified by the Department of the Environment as a Tier III,
outstanding natural resource water body and its watershed, and
[6]
A nontidal wetland of special State concern, as set forth in
COMAR 26.23.01.01 and .04 and COMAR 26.23.06 and
Adverse impacts on water quality that would likely result from the facility
or activity, such as nonpoint source pollution, sewage discharge, or other
pollution related to vessel maintenance
§ 125-70. Industrial and port-related facilities.
In addition to meeting the requirements of § 125-68 and § 125-69, new, expanded, or
redeveloped industrial or port-related facilities or activities and the replacement of these facilities
or activities may be permitted only in those portions of Intensely Developed Areas that have been
designated as Modified Buffer Areas as described in this Chapter and are subject to the
provisions set forth in that Chapter.
§ 125-71. Commercial marinas and other water-dependent commercial maritime facilities
and activities.
A.
In addition to meeting the requirements of § 125-68 and § 125-69:
(1)
A new or expanded commercial marina or related commercial maritime facility or
activity may be permitted in the Buffer of an IDA or LDA;
(2)
A redeveloped or expanded commercial marina or related commercial maritime
facility or activity may be permitted in the Buffer of an RCA or
A new commercial marina or related commercial maritime facility or activity may
be permitted in the Buffer of an RCA only if it is publicly owned and meets all the
requirements of § 125-76.

<!-- page 199 -->
The County shall require that the operation of each commercial marina and each related
commercial maritime facility or activity comply with:
(1)
The requirements of
COMAR 26.08.04.09 and, as applicable, COMAR
26.24.04.03; and
(2)
The stormwater, wastewater, noncontact cooling water discharge, and any other
applicable requirements of the Department of the Environment.
§ 125-72. Community piers and other community boat-docking and storage facilities.
A.
In addition to meeting the requirements of § 125-68 and § 125-69, a new or expanded
community pier or other community boat-docking and storage facilities may be permitted
in the Buffer of an IDA, LDA, or RCA if:
(1)
The owner or operator of the pier or facility:
(a)
Does not offer food, fuel, or other goods and services for sale in the Buffer
or on the community pier; and
(b)
As applicable, complies with the requirements of COMAR 26.24.04.03;
The pier or facility is community-owned, established, and operated for the benefit
of the residents of a platted and recorded riparian subdivision;
The pier or facility is associated with a residential project approved by the County
for the Critical Area and consistent with all State requirements and the
requirements of this Chapter;
Disturbance to the Buffer is the minimum necessary to provide a single point of
access to the pier or facility, and
(5)
If community piers are provided as part of a new residential project, private piers
in the development are not allowed.
The number of slips authorized at a pier or facility shall be the lesser of (a) or (b) below:
(1)
One slip for each 50 feet of shoreline in a residential project in the Intensely
Developed and Limited Development Areas, and one slip for each 300 feet of
shoreline in a residential project in the Resource Conservation Area; or
A density of slips to platted lots or dwellings within a residential project in the
Critical Area according to the following schedule:

§ 125-73. Public beaches and other public water-oriented recreation or education areas, or
activities.
A.
In addition to meeting the requirements of § 125-68 and § 125-69, public beaches or other
public water-oriented recreation or education areas or activities may be permitted in the
Buffer of:
(1)
An Intensely Developed Area; or
(2)
A Limited Development Area or a Resource Conservation Area, provided that:
(a)
Adequate sanitary facilities exist;
(b)
Sanitary and service facilities are, to the extent possible, located outside
the Buffer;
(c)
Permeable surfaces are used to the extent practicable if no degradation of
groundwater would likely result; and
(d)
Disturbance to natural vegetation is first avoided or, if unavoidable,
minimized.
Areas for public passive outdoor recreation, such as nature study, hiking, hunting,
trapping, and for education, may be permitted in the Buffer within a Limited
Development Area or a Resource Conservation Area if sanitary and service facilities for
these uses are located outside of the Buffer.
§ 125-74. Research-associated and education-associated water-dependent facilities or
activities.
In addition to meeting the requirements of § 125-68 and § 125-69, a research-
associated water-dependent facility or activity or an education-associated water-
dependent facility or activity may be permitted in the Buffer of an IDA, LDA, or RCA
if any associated non-water-dependent project or activity is located outside the Buffer.
§ 125-75. Aquaculture and fishery facilities and activities: water quality restoration.
In addition to meeting the requirements of § 125-68 and § 125-69, the following types of
facilities and activities may be permitted in the Buffer of an IDA, LDA, or RCA:
A.
A shore-based facility or activity necessary for
a commercial aquaculture operation;
74

<!-- page 201 -->
A commercial water-dependent fishery facility or activity, including a structure for
crab shedding, a fish off-loading dock, anda shellfish culture operation; and
A facility or activity that supports water quality restoration in the Chesapeake Bay,
the Atlantic Coastal Bays, or their watersheds.
§ 125-76. Nonwater-dependent structures on piers.
A.
Except as provided in paragraphs B
- C of this subsection and notwithstanding any other
provisions of the law, the County will not issue a building permit or any other approval to
authorize a non-water-dependent project constructed or established on State or private
wetlands within the Critical Area.
The County may issue a building permit or any other approval to authorize a non-water-
dependent project on State or private wetlands within the Critical Area if the project:
(1)
Involves a commercial activity that is permitted as a secondary or accessory use to
a permitted primary commercial use;
(2)
Is not located on a pier that is attached to residentially, institutionally, or
industrially used property;
(3)
Is located in an Intensely Developed Area or an area that has been excluded from
Chapter 125 if the exclusion has been adopted or approved by the Critical Area
Commission for the Chesapeake and Atlantic Coastal Bays;
(4)
Is approved by the Planning Commission and the Zoning Board after [date of
Chapter 125 adoption];
(5)
Allows or enhances public access to State wetlands;
(6)
Does not expand beyond the length, width, or channelward encroachment of the
pier on which the project is constructed;
oe)
Has a height of up to 18 feet unless the project is located at a marina; and
(8)
Is up to 1,000 square feet in total area;
The County may issue a building permit or any other approval to authorize a non-water-
dependent project located on State or private wetlands within the Critical Area if the
project:
(1)
Is located on a pier that was in existence on or before December 31, 2012;
(2)
Satisfies all of the requirements under Section B(1)-(7) of this paragraph; and
(3)
If applicable, has a temporary or permanent roof structure or covering that is up to
1,000 square feet in total area.

<!-- page 202 -->
The County may issue a building permit or other approval to authorize a non-water
dependent project for a small-scale renewable energy system ona pier located on State or
private wetlands within the Critical Area if the project:
()
Involves the installation or placement of a small-scale renewable energy system
that is permitted as a secondary or accessory use on a pier that is authorized under
Title 16 of the Environment Article;
Is approved by the Planning Commission after [Date of NWD opt-in];
A building permit or other approval may include the installation or placement of:
(a)
A solar energy system attached to a pier if the device or equipment
associated with that system does not extend more than four (4) feet above
or 18 inches below the deck of the pier or one (1) foot beyond the length or
width of the pier;
A solar energy system attached toa piling if there is only one solar panel
per boat slip;
A solar energy system attached to a boathouse roof if the device or
equipment associated with that system does not extend beyond the length,
width, or height of the boathouse roof,
A closed-loop geothermal heat exchanger undera pier if the geothermal
heat exchanger or any associated devices or equipment do not extend
beyond the length, width, or channelward encroachment of the pier;
deleteriously alter longshore drift or cause significant individual or
cumulative thermal impacts to aquatic resources; or
A wind energy system attached to a pier if there is only one wind energy
system per pier for which the height from the deck of the pier to the blade
extended at its highest point is up to 12 feet; the rotor diameter of the wind
turbine is up to four (4) feet; and the setbacks of the wind energy system
from the nearest property line and from the channelward edge of the pier
to which that system is attached are at least 1.5 times the total height of the
system from its base to the blade extended at its highest point.
Article XV. Shore Erosion Protection.
§ 125-77. Policies.
All shore stabilization activities or projects shall meet the requirements of COMAR 26.24.02 and
COMAR 26.24.04.

<!-- page 203 -->
§ 125-78. Process.
A.
The County requires any authorized shore stabilization project to submit a Buffer
Management Plan.
The Buffer Management Plan shall meet the requirements of § 125-22 of this Chapter and
COMAR 27.01.09.
A copy of the approved Buffer Management Plan will be forwarded to the Critical Area
Commission.
Article XVI. Forest and Woodland Protection.
§ 125-79. General policies.
The following policies for forest and woodland protection recognize the value of forested land for
its water quality benefits and habitat protection while accommodating the utilization of forest
resources:
é
f
fi
.
Ff
.
A.
Maintain and increase the forested and developed woodland vegetation in the Critical
Area;
Conserve forests and developed woodlands and provide for the expansion of forested
areas;
Provide that the removal of trees associated with development activities is minimized and,
where appropriate, mitigated and
Recognize that forests are a protective land use to be managed so that maximum values
for wildlife, water quality, timber, recreation, and other resources are maintained, even
when they are mutually exclusive.
§ 125-80. Standards.
A.
If a forest is to be developed or harvested, a site-specific field investigation shall be
conducted to determine if important sensitive species are present and to ensure that
appropriate protection measures are incorporated into the development plan or Timber
Harvest Plan. Applications shall be forwarded to the Department of Natural Resources,
Wildlife and Heritage Service (WHS) for environmental review. In addition to the below,
any recommendations made by WHS shall be incorporated into the development plan or
Timber Harvest Plan:
(1)
The replacement or establishment of a forest or developed woodlands should
ensure a diversified plant community and should include canopy trees, understory
trees, shrubs, and herbaceous plants.
Native species should be used for all reforestation and afforestation.
77

<!-- page 204 -->
When proposing development activities within riparian forests or forest areas utilized as
breeding areas by forest interior dwelling birds, applicants shall utilize the guidance
found in the Critical Area Commission publication entitled,
A Guide to the Conservation
ofForest Interior Dwelling Birds in the Chesapeake Bay Critical Area, dated June 2000,
and as may be subsequently amended.
The Department ofNatural Resources may make specific recommendations based on an
evaluation of the site and the proposed development, including:
(1)
Minimize forest and woodland disturbance from off-road vehicles, public use, or
logging from May through August of each year;
(2)
Focus all development on the periphery of the forest or woodlands;
(3)
Retain the forest canopy as well as the shrub understory;
(4)
Retain snag and mature seed trees as dens for woodpeckers and as nests for bald
eagles;
(5)
Discourage the creation of small clearings and expansion of forest edge habitats;
(6)
Encourage re-establishment of native forests and woodlands; and
(7)
Adopt harvest techniques to maintain or improve habitat.
§ 125-81. Commercial timber harvesting.
A.
Landowners proposing to harvest timber within any one-year interval and affecting one
or more acres in the Critical Area shall follow a "Timber Harvest Plan.” A registered
professional forester shall prepare this plan. The Timber Harvest Plan shall be reviewed
and approved by the Department of Natural Resources through the District Forestry
Board. The approved plan shall be filed with the County Soil Conservation District, and a
copy of the approved plan shall be sent to the County Planning, Zoning, and Community
Development Department prior to commencing timbering operations. The office shall
review the plan to ensure its consistency with this Chapter.
Any landowner who plans to harvest timber on an area that will disturb 5,000 square feet
or more, including harvesting on agricultural lands, shall follow a Sediment Control Plan
in accordance with specifications set out by the Department of Natural Resources and
enforced by the Department of the Environment and the County.
Timber harvests are permitted in the Buffer in accordance with the provisions of COMAR
27.01.09.01-7.
§ 125-82. Enforcement.
Unauthorized clearing, cutting, or removal of vegetation; unauthorized clearing, cutting, or
removal of vegetation in the Buffer; and clearing, cutting, or removal of vegetation in excess of
78

<!-- page 205 -->
the area permitted to be cleared by this Chapter is considered a violation of this Chapter and shall
result in enforcement action as specified in Article II of this Chapter.
Article
XVIL. Agriculture.
§ 125-83. General policies.
The County will follow all ofthe following policies with regard to agriculture in the Critical
Area:
A.
Assure that agricultural lands are identified and that programs are established for the
Critical Area to maintain, where appropriate, agricultural lands in agricultural use to the
greatest extent possible, and
Recognize that agriculture is a protective land use that should be properly managed so
that it minimizes its contribution to pollutant loadings to the Chesapeake Bay and
Atlantic coastal bays and their tributaries.
§ 125-84. Criteria.
A.
The County will develop an agricultural protection plan in cooperation with the County
Soil Conservation District, the County agricultural land preservation advisory board, and
any other appropriate agency.
Each agricultural protection plan developed shall:

Consist of:

An identification, inventory, and mapping of agricultural land within the
Critical Area;
An identification of agricultural land that includes a habitat protection area
covered in COMAR 27.01.09 and
A program to maintain agricultural land in agricultural use and to protect
water quality and plant and wildlife habitat, which shall at least include:

Measures to encourage the preservation of agricultural land;
In accordance with the provisions of COMAR 27.01.09, protection
of a habitat protection area located within agricultural land and
[3]
In order to conform to harvest practice requirements in COMAR
27.01.05 and 27.01.09.01-7, a timber harvest plan and
Assure that:
[1]
Each agricultural operation is in place and is implementing a
current:

[2]
[3]
Soil conservation and water quality plan; and
Nutrient management plan prepared bya certified nutrient
management consultant or certified farm operator in accordance
with COMAR 15.20.04.07 and .08;
Agricultural activities authorized in the Buffer are in accordance with
COMAR 27.01.09.01-6 and
The creation of new agricultural land is not accomplished:
[1]
By diking, draining, or filling of any class or subclass of palustrine
wetlands, as described in this program, which has a seasonally
flooded or wetter water regime, unless mitigation is accomplished
in accordance with applicable State and County regulations;
By clearing forests or woodlands on soils with a slope greater than
15 percent or on soils with a "K" value greater than 0.35 anda

[a]
If the clearing will adversely affect water quality or will
destroy plant and wildlife habitat as defined in this Chapter,
or
[b]
By the clearing of existing natural vegetation within the
Buffer as defined in this Chapter.
Article
XVILL. Surface Mining.
§ 125-85. Applicability.
A.
The provisions of this Chapter apply to all existing and proposed operations for which a
license, permit, or other approval was issued on or after October 13, 1989, including
those engaged in the extraction or removal of minerals, sand, gravel, rock, stone, earth, or
fill, and activities related to surface mining. These activities include, but are not limited
to, operations engaged in processing minerals at the site, including removal and mining
for prospecting, washing mined material, and loading and transporting mined material.
Notwithstanding any other provision of law, the provisions of this subsection may not be
construed to prohibit the location or use of a water-dependent facility, in accordance with
COMAR 27.01.03 or Article XIV of this Chapter, for the transport of a surface mining
product or by-product.
§ 125-86. General policies.
Wicomico County shall ensure that due consideration is accorded to the following:
A.
When locating a surface mining activity, avoidance or, in the alternative, minimization of:
80

<!-- page 207 -->
(1)
The substantial loss of renewable resource land; and
(2)
The degradation of water quality; and
When conducting a surface mining activity:
(1)
Utilization of all available measures to protect the Critical Area from all sources
of pollution resulting from that activity, including sedimentation and siltation,
chemical and petrochemical use and spillage, and storage or disposal of waste,
dust, or spoil; and
(2)
Facilitation of site reclamation, including renewable resource land, as soon as
possible and to the maximum extent possible.
§ 125-87. Standards.
Proposed new surface mining sites or expanded operations of existing sites shall comply with the
following:
A.
The following areas shall not be used for surface mining:
(1)
Habitat Protection Areas;
(2)
Areas where highly erodible soils exist; and
(3)
An area that is within 100 feet immediately landward from the mean high water of
tidal waters or the edge of a bank of a tributary stream.
Surface mining operations shall operate under the following conditions:
(1)
Future wash plants, including ponds, spoil piles, and equipment, may not be
located within the Buffer in accordance with COMAR 27.01.09.
To the fullest extent possible, existing sand and gravel operations shall conduct
their extraction activities so as to provide, at a minimum, a 100-foot Buffer of
natural vegetation between the operation and the mean high-water line of tidal
waters or the edges of streams and tidal wetlands, whichever is further inland.
Article XIX. Renewable Energy Generating Systems.
§ 125-88. Applicability.
A.
The requirements of this Article are applicable to major, minor, and small renewable
energy generating systems located in the Critical Area.
In accordance with COMAR 27.01.14.02, these provisions may not be construed to limit
the authority of the Public Service Commission under Public Utilities Article §7-207,
Annotated Code of Maryland.

<!-- page 208 -->
§ 125-89. Application requirements.
A.
An applicant for a major solar energy generating system in a Resource Conservation Area
shall provide the County with a site plan that addresses the requirements of COMAR
27.02.07 and includes the following:
(1)
Calculations of any required reservation of Resource Conservation Area density
development rights; and
The identification and location of property restrictions, including the boundary
and area of a conservation easement, restrictive covenant, or other protective
instrument.
B.
The County may requirea site plan for a minor solar energy generating system.
§ 125-90. General provisions.
A.
On or after April 1, 2021, the County may authorize:
(1)
A major or minor solar energy generating system within theCritical Areain
accordance with this subsection;
A major solar energy generating system in the Resource Conservation Area
without growth allocation in accordance with § 125-91; and
A small residential accessory solar energy generating system in the Buffer or a
Modified Buffer Area if:
(a)
There is no alternative location outside the Buffer;
(b)
The lot is 1/4 acre or less in size and created before October 13, 1989; and
(9)
Mitigation is required at a 1:1 ratio.
A major and minor solar energy generating system may be authorized in a modified
buffer area, provided the project is located over existing legally developed lot coverage.
In addition to meeting the requirements of this Article, the County may not authorize a
growth allocation to accommodate a major or a minor solar energy generating system in
the Limited Development Area or the Resource Conservation Area.
Except for a regulated activity that the Maryland Department of the Environment
authorizes in accordance with COMAR 26.23.02, the County may not authorize a major
or minor solar energy generating system:
(1)
In a habitat protection area designated under COMAR 27.01.09, or
(2)
Ona steep slope or a highly erodible soil.

<!-- page 209 -->
In accordance with
COMAR 27.01.14, a variance, modification, waiver, or other approval
that alters the requirements of this Article may not be granted.
§ 125-91. Criteria for a solar energy generating system.
Except for a small residential accessory solar energy generating system, the provisions of
this section are applicable to a major and minor solar energy generating system in an
intensely developed area, a limited development area, and a resource conservation area:
A.
The area of a solar panel shall not count as lot coverage and:
(1)
For a minor solar energy generating system, the solar panel shall be:
(a)
Located over existing, legally developed lot coverage as described in
Natural Resources Article, §8-1808.3, Annotated Code of Maryland; or
(b)
Elevated above the ground and the area under the solar panel is maintained
as an area of existing grass, established grass, or other natural vegetation,
or as an agricultural use; and
For a major solar energy generating system, the solar panel shall be:
é))
Located over an existing, legally developed lot coverage or
(b)
Elevated above the ground, and the area under the solar panel is
maintained in accordance with the planting plan requirements § 125-93 as:
[1]
Pollinator habitat;
[2|
Native vegetation other than pollinator habitat; or
[3]
An agricultural use.
The Buffer shall be measured and delineated in accordance with
COMAR
27.01.09.01.E(3)-(7) and Article IV of this Chapter and:
(1)
Unless there is no feasible alternative, access through the Buffer to the project area
is prohibited;
When there is no feasible alternative to access through the buffer, one point of
access through the Buffer to the project area may be authorized. Where a project
includes noncontiguous parcels, the number of access points through the Buffer
shall be minimized;
(3)
Mitigation for disturbance to the Buffer is required at a 2.5:1] ratio; and
(4)
A buffer management plan shall be provided in accordance with COMAR
27.01.09.01-3.
Except whena project area is in an IDA, clearing of forest and developed woodlands shall
be minimized and not exceed the following:
83

<!-- page 210 -->
In an LDA, clearing of forest and developed woodlands is limited to 20 percent of
the entirety of the forest and developed woodlands on the parcel or parcels on
which the project area is located; and
In an RCA, clearing of forest and developed woodlands is limited to 10 acres or
20 percent of the entirety of the forest and developed woodlands on the parcel or
parcels on which the project area is located, whichever is less.
In an IDA, LDA, and RCA, mitigation for cleared forest and developed woodlands shall
be provided on at least an equal area basis.
For a major solar energy generating system, on-site or off-site planting shall be in
accordance with the following:
(1)
15 percent of the total project area in a limited development area; and
(2)
20 percent of the total project area is in a resource conservation area.
A planting plan shall be provided in accordance with § 125-93.
Stormwater management shall be provided ‘in accordance with the Environment Article,
§§4-201-4-215, Annotated Code of Maryland, and COMAR 26.17.02.
A decommissioning plan shall be required if one is not otherwise required as a result of
obtaining a Certificate of Public Convenience and Necessity from the Public Service
Commission.
§ 125-92. Criteria for a major solar energy generating system in the Resource Conservation
Area.
These provisions apply to a major solar energy generating system in the RCA in addition to
the requirements under § 125-9] above:
A.
Except for access allowed in accordance with the provisions in COMAR 27.01.06, forest
clearing shall be restricted within 300 feet landward from the mean high-water line of
tidal waters, from the landward boundary ofa tidal wetland, or the edge of each bank of a
tributary stream.
In addition to any applicable local land recordation requirements, a Reservation of
Resource Conservation Area Density Rights Agreement shall be recorded in the County
land records that reserves RCA density rights associated with the project as follows:
(i)
The number of density rights reserved is equal to:
(a)
The permitted RCA density associated with the project area of each parcel
as calculated under Natural Resources Article, §8- 1808.1(e), Annotated
Code of Maryland, and COMAR 27.01.02.05C(4); and

<!-- page 211 -->
(b)
The number of density rights that are attributable to the project area, with a
minimum of one density right reserved.
The Reservation of Resource Conservation Area Density Rights Agreement shall remain
in effect until:
(1)
The decommissioning plan, as required in § 125-91 H, has been implemented and
completed; and
(2)
The termination of the Reservation of Resource Conservation Area Density Rights
Agreement is reflected in the land records.
The remaining land unencumbered by the solar energy generating system or lands not
otherwise restricted by the Reservation of Resource Conservation Area Density Rights
Agreement may be developed in accordance with Natural Resources Article, §8-1808.1,
Annotated Code of Maryland, and COMAR 27.01.02.05C(4).
A lot, parcel, or portion of a lot or parcel is not eligible for a major solar energy
generating system if the density rights associated with that lot, parcel, or portion of a lot
or parcel have been:

(1)
Utilized for an intrafamily transfer;
A)
Transferred through a transfer of development rights program;

(4)
Otherwise reserved in association with an area of land to be utilized for the solar
energy generating system.

Replanting required in § 125-91.

Contiguous to or within a designated forest interior dwelling bird habitat;

A.
B.

The fee shall be held in a separate account independent of other Critical Area funds,
which may not revert to the general fund;
The fee shall not be used to meet other landscaping requirements, and

Off-site plantings in accordance with the options provided in § 125-93B(1) or

Article XX. Special Site Plan Provisions.
§ 125-95. Purpose.
The purpose of the site plan is to ensure detailed compliance with applicable provisions of this
Chapter and to prescribe standards for the design and construction of site improvements.
Development requiring site plan approval shall be permitted only in accordance with all the
specifications contained in an approved site plan. It shall not be undertaken until the site plan is
approved and all the required construction permits have been obtained subsequent to such
approval.
§ 125-96. Approving authority.
A.*
— Site plans for development activities required in this Chapter shall be prepared and
submitted as described below and approved by the Wicomico County Planning and
Zoning Commission.
Site plans for all multifamily dwellings, industrial and commercial buildings and
developments, and mobile home parks shall be prepared and approved as set forth in the
Wicomico County Zoning Chapter and Subdivision Chapter, as amended, in addition to
the requirements of this Chapter.
The Planning Director or designee shall approve site plans accompanying individual
building permit applications.
